### Possible Test Day Questions

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The primary objective of my program is to provide both entertainment and education to the user by teaching them about geography.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The program asks the user for a state and the corresponding capital. The code will find the index of where the state is in the state list, then compare the capital listed at the same index, but in the capital list. If the found capital matches the user’s inputted capital, the phrase “You are correct” is displayed. If the capital in the list does not match the user’s chosen capital, the phrase “You are incorrect” is displayed. If the state does not exist, the phrase “Your state does not exist” is displayed.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

The user is prompted for the state. The user inputs “Florida”.

The user is prompted for the capital. The user inputs “Tallahassee”.

The program outputs to the screen “You are correct”.

4.Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data in my list are strings that represent all 50 states.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently, if you did not use the list.

By using my list, “listOfStates,” I only had to name one variable—the list itself. If I did not use my list, I would have to create and name all 50 strings contained in the list. My list called “listOfStates” reduced the level of complexity by reducing the amount of code I needed to write. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chances for errors. Using my list called “listOfStates” allowed me to access the strings in the list by index. If I did not use my list, I would have to access the strings by their names. Combining the index with a for loop allowed me to test each element using one if statement. If I did not use a list, I would need to use 50 if statements. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chances for errors.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

My procedure takes in a 50-element list containing state names as well as a 50-element list containing state capital names. The user selects a state name and a capital name. The procedure then determines in what index the state variable is located in the list, “listOfStates.” The procedure will then check to see if that index in the second list contains the same capital name as the user-selected capital name. If they are the same, the user is correct. If they are different, the user is incorrect. If the state is not in “listOfStates,” the message “Your state does not exist” is displayed.

7.Describe in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

The procedure takes in two lists, called “listOfStates” and “listOfCapitals”, as well as two strings, called state and capital.

■Sets the value of index to −1.

■Sets the value of count to 0.

■Iterates through the list “listOfStates” using a for each loop.

∘If the element of “listOfStates” is equal to state, the value of index is set to count.

∘Adds one to count.

■Ends the for each loop.

■If index is still set to −1, display the message “Your state does not exist.”

■If the variable capital is equal to the element contained in “listOfCapitals” at the found index, display the message “You are correct.”

■If the variable capital is not equal to the element contained in “listOfCapitals” at the found index, display the message “You are incorrect.”

8.Describe two calls to the procedure identified in written response 3i. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

Assume “listOfStates” contains “Alabama”, “Alaska”, “Arizona”, “Arkansas”, “California”, “Colorado”, “Connecticut”, “Delaware”, and “Florida,” and “listOfCapitals” contains “Montgomery”, “Juneau”, “Phoenix”, “Little Rock”, “Sacramento”, “Denver”, “Hartford”, “Dover”, and “Tallahassee.”

state is “Alaska”

capital is “Juneau”

Call to procedure would be: playCapitalGame (“listOfStates”, “listOfCapitals”, Alaska, Juneau)

Second call:

Assume “listOfStates” contains “Alabama”, “Alaska”, “Arizona”, “Arkansas”, “California”, “Colorado”, “Connecticut”, “Delaware”, and “Florida,” and “listOfCapitals” contains “Montgomery”, “Juneau”, “Phoenix”, “Little Rock”, “Sacramento”, “Denver”, “Hartford”, “Dover”, and “Tallahassee.”

state is “Alaska”

capital is “Dover”

Call to procedure would be: playCapitalGame (“listOfStates”, “listOfCapitals”, Alaska, Dover)

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

Correct Answer:

Condition(s) tested by the first call:

Is the capital of Alaska Juneau?

Condition(s) tested by the second call:

Is the capital of Alaska Dover?

Identify the result of each call.

Correct Answer:

Result of the first call:

“You are correct”

Result of the second call:

“You are incorrect”

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for 50 iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

The loop utilized in my program is a “for-each” loop, which inherently commences at the first index and concludes at the last index, preventing the possibility of an infinite loop. Nonetheless, if I were to employ a “while” loop and continue it until a state is found in the list, and that state is not actually present in the list, it would lead to an infinite loop.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Trying to access an array element with an index that falls beyond the permissible range of indices will result in a runtime error. For instance, in the “playCapitalGame” procedure, the “listOfCapitals” list comprises 50 elements indexed from 0 to 49. An attempt to access an element at index 51 would trigger a runtime error. If the “for-each” loop were changed to a “REPEAT 52 TIMES” loop command, it would surpass the valid boundaries of the “listOfCapitals” list, leading to a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Changing the “if” statement from “IF x == state” to “IF x != state” when searching for the state in the “listOfStates” list would introduce a logic error.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

Setting the initial value of `count` as “count + 1 = count” rather than “count = count + 1” in the code would result in a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

If the data type of `count` were an 8-bit integer, the maximum value it could hold without triggering an overflow error would be 2^n − 1, which, in this specific case, is 256 − 1, equal to 255. Consequently, attempting to store the number 278 in `count` would result in an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm responsible for checking the presence of a state in a list within a procedure, I have significantly simplified the complexity of my program. This simplification allows me to reuse the algorithm at various points in my program without the need for code duplication. For instance, if I need to verify the presence of five states in my list, I can simply call the procedure five times. Without this procedural approach, I would be obliged to replicate the entire code five times instead of just once.

Furthermore, the utilization of procedures ensures that errors related to state presence checks are confined within the procedure itself. Without procedures, I would have to inspect the entire program to locate the source of any errors related to this functionality.

16.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called isEqual(value1, value2). The procedure returns true if the two parameters, value1 and value2, are equal in value and returns false otherwise. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses isEqual to count the number of times a certain value appears in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

1.**Initialization**: Start by initializing the variable “total” with a value of 0.

2.**Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “listOfStates” list.

3.**Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is equal to “state.” If a match is found, increment the “total” by one.

4.**Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

![Images](OEBPS/images/f084-01.jpg)

17.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called compareTo(value1, value2). The procedure returns a negative number if value1 has a lower alphabetical order than value2, a positive value if value1 has a higher alphabetical order than value 2, and returns 0 if value1 has the same alphabetical order as value2. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses compareTo to count the number of times the word stored in the value user appears before each item in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

1.**Initialization**: Begin by setting the initial value of the “total” variable to 0.

2.**Iteration Through the List**: Initiate a loop that will iterate through the entire “listOfStates.”

3.**Alphabetical Comparison**: When using the “compareTo” operation to compare “value” with an item from the “listOfStates,” if it returns a positive value, it indicates that “user” has a higher alphabetical order than the current list item.

In such a case, increment the “total” variable by one.

4.**Repeat for All Items**: Continue to perform step 3 for the entire “listOfStates” list, repeating this process fifty times.

5.**End the Loop**: Conclude the loop.

6.**Result Accumulation**: Return the cumulative value of the “total.”

These steps outline a process to compare “user” with each item in the “listOfStates” list, incrementing the “total” for each instance where “user” is found to have a higher alphabetical order.

18.Suppose you have access to a procedure named conCat(word1, word2), which concatenates two words. For example, if you call conCat(“Tiger”, “Cat”), it returns “TigerCat”. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses conCat to return a single word that contains all the words in your list combined into one large word. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

1.**Initialization**: Begin by setting the initial value of the “combinedWord” to an empty string.

2.**Iteration Through the List**: Initiate a loop that will traverse the entire “listOfStates.”

3.**Concatenation**: During each iteration, concatenate the “combinedWord” with the current item in the list.

4.**Repeat for All Items**: Continue to perform step 3 for the entire “listOfStates” list, repeating this process fifty times.

5.**End the Loop**: Conclude the loop.

6.**Result Accumulation**: Return the accumulated value of the “combinedWord.”

These steps describe a process to concatenate the items from the “listOfStates” list to form a single, combined word, which is then returned as the result.

### Possible Test Day Questions

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The purpose of my program is to entertain users while they learn a practical application of statistics.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The user first selected the number of dice to roll. In the video, the user picked 100 dice. The dice are numbered 1 to 10. The user then picked their lucky number and predicted how many times that number occurred in the dice rolls. In the video the user picked 5 for their lucky number and 100 for the number of times the number appears in the list. If the user guessed the number correctly, the message “You win” was displayed. If the user did not guess correctly, the message “You lose” was displayed.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

The user is asked how many dice they want to roll. Input: The user entered 100.

The user is asked to enter their lucky number. Input: The user entered 5.

The user is asked how many times their lucky number appears. Input: The user entered 100.

Output: The phrase “You lose” is displayed.

4.Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data in my list are random integers that represent dice rolls from 1 to 10.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently, if you did not use the list.

My list “diceRollList” held 100 integers. By using a list, I only had to name one variable. If I did not use a list, I would have to name all 100 variables. By reducing the required code, I reduced the chances for errors, thus reducing the level of complexity of my program. By using my list “diceRollList,” if I wanted to change the size of the list from 100 to 200, no additional code would be required. If I did not use a list, I would have to name an additional 100 variables. By reducing the required code, I reduced the chances for errors, thus reducing the level of complexity of my program. My list “diceRollList” allowed me to access my dice rolls by index instead of by name. Using a for loop and index allowed me to test all my integers to see if they were equal to the lucky number using only one selection statement. If I did not use a list, my code would require 100 selection statements. By reducing the required code, I reduced the chances for errors, thus reducing the level of complexity of my program.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

My procedure playGame takes in the list of dice rolls, the user’s lucky number, and a guess on how many times the lucky number appears. Each dice roll is checked, and the code counts how many times the lucky number appears in the list. If the counted total is equal to the guess, the user wins. If the counted total is not equal to the guess, the user loses.

7.Describe in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

Line 7: My procedure playGame takes in the list “diceRollList”, the integer luckyNumber, and the integer numAppear.

Line 8: Sets the counter variable total to 0.

Lines 9–11: Uses a for each loop to iterate through the list. If the value in the list is equal to luckyNumber, add one to total.

Line 12: If total is equal to numAppear output to the screen, “You Win.”

Line 13: If total is not equal to numAppear output to the screen, “You Lose.”

8.Describes two calls to the procedure identified in written response 3i. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

Assuming diceRollList contained the numbers \[1, 1, 3, 5, 6\], the lucky number was 1, and numAppear was 2.

playGame(diceRollList, luckyNumber, numAppear) would be playGame(\[1, 1, 3, 5, 6\], 1, 2\]

Second call:

Assuming diceRollList contained the numbers \[1, 1, 3, 5, 6\], the lucky number was 1, and numAppear was 3.

playGame(diceRollList, luckyNumber, numAppear) would be playGame(\[1, 1, 3, 5, 6\], 1, 3\]

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

Condition(s) tested by the first call:

The procedure is testing if the number 1 is contained two times in the list.

Condition(s) tested by the second call:

The procedure is testing if the number 1 is contained three times in the list.

Identify the result of each call.

Result of the first call:

“You win”

Result of the second call:

“You lose”

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for an inputted value, which in this case was 100 iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

The loop employed in my program is a “for-each” loop, which automatically starts at the first index and naturally terminates at the last index, ensuring the prevention of an infinite loop. However, if I were to switch to using a “while” loop and set it to continue until a dice roll of 15 occurs, it would lead to an infinite loop. This is because the range of a dice roll is from 1 to 10, and a roll of 15 will never occur, resulting in an infinite loop.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Attempting to access an array element with an index that exceeds the allowed range of indices will lead to a runtime error. For example, within the “playGame” procedure, the “diceRollList” consists of 1,000 elements indexed from 0 to 999. Trying to access an element at index 1005 would result in a runtime error. If the “for-each” loop were replaced with a “REPEAT 1000 TIMES” loop command, it would exceed the valid boundaries of the “diceRollsList” list, causing a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Altering the code on line 11 from “total = total + 1” to “total = total + 2” would introduce a logical error. The outcomes would be twice the intended results.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

Omitting the “:” at the end of the if statement in line 10 would result in a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

It’s quite challenging to encounter an overflow error when working with Python due to its dynamic typing, as values are not restricted to a fixed number of bits. However, if the data type of “total” were an 8-bit integer, the highest value it could contain without causing an overflow error would be 2^n − 1, which, in this particular case, equals 255 (as in 256 − 1). Therefore, any attempt to store the number 278 in “total” would indeed lead to an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm responsible for counting the occurrences of a lucky number in the “diceRollList” within a procedure, I have substantially reduced the complexity of my program. This streamlining allows me to reuse the algorithm at multiple points in my program without the necessity for code redundancy. For example, when I require confirming the presence of a lucky number in ten different dice rolls, I can simply call the procedure ten times. Without this procedural approach, I would need to duplicate the entire code ten times instead of just once.

Additionally, the utilization of procedures ensures that errors pertaining to lucky number checks are localized within the procedure itself. In the absence of procedures, I would have to examine the entire program to pinpoint the source of any errors related to this functionality.

2.**Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “diceRollList” list.

3.**Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is equal to “luckyNumber” If a match is found, increase the “total” by one.

4.**Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

### **Possible Test Day Questions**

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The purpose of my program is to entertain the user while teaching them statistics.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The program asks the user how many numbers they want to play with. The user then guesses a number. If that number is contained in the list, the number is removed from the list, and the user scores 10 points. If the user guesses a number that is not contained in the list, the game is over, and the total points won is displayed. The game continues until the user guesses a number not contained in the list.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

1\. The user is prompted to enter how many numbers they want to play with. The user inputs 16. The user is prompted to pick a number. The user inputs 3. The program outputs to the screen 2. The user is prompted to pick a number. The user inputs 6. The program outputs to the screen 3. The user is prompted to pick a number. The user inputs 6. The program outputs to the screen the following:

−1

Game Over.

Your score is 20

4. Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data in my list are random integers from 0 to 10 that represent the numbers. used for the guessing game.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently, if you did not use the list.

By using my list “listOfNumbers,” I only had to name one variable—the list itself.

If I did not use my list, I would have to create and name all 16 integers contained in the list. My list called “listOfNumbers” reduced the level of complexity by reducing the amount of code I needed to write. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chances for errors.

Using my list called “listOfNumbers” allowed me to access the numbers in the list by index. If I did not use the list, I would have to access the numbers by their names. Combining the index with a for loop allowed me to test each element using one if statement. If I did not use a list, I would need to use 16 if statements. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chances for errors.

By using my list “listOfNumbers,” if I wanted to change the size of the list from 16 to 2000, no additional code would be required. If I did not use a list, I would have to name an additional 1984 variables. By reducing the required code, I reduced the chances for errors, thus reducing the level of complexity of my program.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

My procedure takes in a list of random numbers and a number guessed by the user.

The procedure returns the number of times the number appears in the list. If the number is not found, −1 is returned. All occurrences of the guessed number are cleared from the list after being counted.

7.Describe in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

Line 9: The procedure playGame takes in a list of numbers and a number guessed by the user.

Line 10: The variable total is set to 0.

Lines 11–14: The list listOfNumbers is iterated, and if the guessed number is in the list, it is removed, and one is added to total.

Lines 15–16: If total is still equal to 0, −1 is returned.

Lines 17–18: In all other cases, the total number of times the guessed number was found in the list is returned.

8.Describe two calls to the procedure identified in written response 3i. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

Assume listOfNumbers contains \[1, 1, 3, 5, 6, 7\], and the guess is 1.

The call would be playGame(listOfNumbers, 1).

Second call:

Assume listOfNumbers contains \[1, 1, 3, 5, 6, 7\], and the guess is 4.

The call would be playGame(listOfNumbers, 4).

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

Condition(s) tested by the first call:

Is 1 contained in the list? If yes, how many times?

Condition(s) tested by the second call:

Is 4 contained in the list? If yes, how many times?

Identify the result of each call

Result of the first call:

The number 2 is returned.

Result of the second call:

The number −1 is returned.

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for an inputted value which, in this case, was 16 iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

Replacing line 13 of the procedure with “listOfNumbers.append(item)” would trigger an infinite loop due to the continuous expansion of the list, preventing the “for-each” loop from ever concluding.

Additionally, the loop employed in my program is a “for-each” loop, which inherently initiates at the first index and naturally concludes at the last index, ensuring the avoidance of an infinite loop. However, if I were to switch to a “while” loop and set it to continue until “total” is equal to −1, it would result in an infinite loop. This is because the total length of the list can never be a negative number, inevitably leading to an infinite loop.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Trying to access an array element with an index that surpasses the permissible range of indices will result in a runtime error. For instance, in the “playGame” procedure, the “listOfNumbers” contains 16 elements indexed from 0 to 15. Attempting to access an element at index 20 would trigger a runtime error. If the “for-each” loop were substituted with a “REPEAT 20 TIMES” loop command, it would go beyond the valid boundaries of the “diceRollsList” list, resulting in a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Altering the code on line 14 from “total = total + 1” to “total = total + 2” would introduce a logical error. The outcomes would be twice the intended results.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

Removing the indentation from line 13 would also result in a syntax error. Additionally, leaving out the “:” at the end of the “for” statement in line 11 would lead to a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

Experiencing an overflow error in Python can be quite challenging due to its dynamic typing, as values are not constrained to a fixed number of bits. However, if the data type of “total” were an 8-bit integer, the maximum value it could hold without triggering an overflow error would be 2^n – 1, which, in this specific instance, amounts to 255 (derived from 256 – 1). Consequently, any endeavor to store the number 278 in “total” would unquestionably result in an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction, and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm within a procedure, I have significantly reduced the complexity of my program. This simplification enables me to reuse the algorithm at various points in my program without the need for redundant code. For instance, when I need to verify the presence of a number in ten rounds of the game, I can simply call the procedure ten times. Without this procedural approach, I would have to replicate the entire code ten times instead of just once.

Additionally, the utilization of procedures ensures that errors related to the “playGame” functionality are confined within the procedure itself. Without procedures, I would be required to scrutinize the entire program to identify the source of any errors related to this functionality.

16.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called isEqual(value1, value2). The procedure returns true if the two parameters, value1 and value2, are equal in value and returns false otherwise. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses isEqual to count the number of times a certain value appears in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

1.**Initialization**: Start by initializing the variable “total” with a value of 0.

2.**Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “listOfNumbers” list.

3.**Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is compared to a number contained in the list. If a match is found, increase the “total” by one.

4.**Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

### **Possible Test Day Questions**

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The purpose of my program is to allow the user to scramble messages to increase privacy when sending communications.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The program asks the user to enter a phrase. If the user chooses to encrypt the phrase, the phrase will be scrambled based on the key contained in the list keyList. If the key number is even, the program will add the key value to the character. If the key value is odd, the program will add the key value plus two. If the user chooses to decrypt the phrase, the phrase will be unscrambled based on the key contained in the list keyList. If the key number is even, the program will subtract the key value from the character. If the key value is odd, the program will subtract the key value minus two.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

The user is prompted to enter their phrase. The user inputs “Dogs like socks but not on their feet.”

The user is then prompted to indicate if they want the program to encrypt or decrypt.

The user inputs “e.”

The program then outputs Csm.v%mhql”tsfqr”jzz#ruw”sqyphms”jhky6.

4.Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data in my list are integers that represent how much each character in the user’s phrase will be shifted.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently if you did not use the list.

By using my list “keyList,” I only had to name one variable—the list itself. If I did not use my list, I would have to create and name all 46 integers that are contained in the list. “keyList” reduced the level of complexity by reducing the amount of code I needed to write. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chances for errors.

Using my list called “keyList” allowed me to access the integers in the list by index. If I did not use my list, I would have to access the integers by their names. Combining the index with a for loop allowed me to test each element using one if statement. If I did not use a list, I would need to use 46 separate if statements. By reducing the amount of code needed, it reduced the chance for errors.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

My procedure takes in a 46-element list containing integers, as well as a string that indicates if the phrase will be encrypted or decrypted. Each letter in the phrase matches with a corresponding key number. If the key number is even and encryption is selected, the letter is moved forward equal to the key number. If the key number is odd and encryption is selected, the letter is moved forward equal to the key number minus two. If the key number is even and decryption is selected, the letter is moved backward equal to the key number. If the key number is odd and decryption is selected, the letter is moved backward equal to the key number plus two. The new word is then displayed on the screen.

7.Describe in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

Line 4: The procedure scrambleWord takes in a phrase called “word” and a string called “enOrDe”.

Line 5: The empty list tempList is created.

Lines 6–7: A for loop is executed the same size as the length of word and appends each letter the word to a character into the list tempList.

Line 8: A empty string called “result” is created.

Line 9: x is set to 0.

Line 10: A for loop is set up to iterate through the list keyList.

Line 11: If the value of x is less than the length of word and...

Line 12: If the current element of keyList is even and...

Lines 13–14: If enOrDe is set to encrypt shift the character forward the keyList value and is added to result.

Lines 15–16: Else if enOrDe is set to decrypt shift the character backwards the keyList value and is added to result.

Line 17: Else if the current element of keyList is odd and...

Lines 18–19: If enOrDe is set to encrypt shift the character forward the keyList value − 2 and is added to result.

Lines 20–21: Else if enOrDe is set to decrypt shift the character backwards the keyList value + 2 and is added to result.

Line 22: One is added to x.

Line 23: Go to line 11 until the for loop is complete.

Line 24: Displays the value of the translated word in result.

8.Describe two calls to the procedure identified in written response 3i. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

scrambleWord( “Mississippi”, e)

Second call:

scrambleWord(“Lmyzlxthvwk”, d)

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

Condition(s) tested by the first call:

Encrypt the word “Mississippi” based on the stored key.

Condition(s) tested by the second call:

Decrypt the word “Lmyzlxthvwk” based on the stored key.

Identify the result of each call.

Result of the first call:

Lmyzlxthvwk

Result of the second call:

Mississippi

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for the length of the string, which in this case was 11 iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

The loop used in my program is a “for-each” loop, which automatically commences at the first index and naturally terminates at the last index, guaranteeing the prevention of an infinite loop. However, if I were to switch to a “while” loop and configure it to continue until “len(word) == 0,” it would result in an infinite loop. This is because the total length of the “word” would never reach 0, inevitably leading to an infinite loop.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Attempting to perform a division by zero would result in a runtime error. Modifying line 12 to “if (item / 0 == 0)” would also lead to a runtime error.

Moreover, using a variable or procedure that has not been defined would likewise trigger a runtime error. Consequently, changing line 28 from “scrambleWord(word, enOrDe)” to “mixUpWord(word, enOrDe)” would also result in a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Altering the code on line 14 from + item to −item would cause incorrect results and produce a logical error.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

Removing the indentation from line 11 would result in a syntax error. Additionally, leaving out the “:” at the end of the “for” statement in line 10 would lead to a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

Experiencing an overflow error in Python can be quite challenging due to its dynamic typing, as values are not constrained to a fixed number of bits. However, if the data type of “x” were an 8-bit integer, the maximum value it could hold without triggering an overflow error would be 2^n – 1, which, in this specific instance, amounts to 255 (derived from 256 – 1). Consequently, any endeavor to store the number 278 in “x” would unquestionably result in an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction, and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm within a procedure, I have significantly reduced the complexity of my program. This simplification allows me to reuse the algorithm at multiple points in my program without the need for redundant code. For example, when I need to scramble each letter in a word, I can simply call the procedure the length of the word times, which in this case was 11 times. Without this procedural approach, I would have to replicate the entire code 11 times instead of just once.

Furthermore, the use of procedures ensures that errors associated with the “scrambleWord” functionality are localized within the procedure itself. Without procedures, I would need to inspect the entire program to pinpoint the source of any errors related to this functionality.

16.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called isEqual(value1, value2). The procedure returns true if the two parameters, value1 and value2, are equal in value and returns false otherwise. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses isEqual to count the number of times a certain value appears in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

1.**Initialization**: Start by initializing the variable “total” with a value of 0.

2.**Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “keyList” list.

3.**Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is compared to a number contained in the list. If a match is found, increase the “total” by one.

4.**Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

### **Graphical-Based Language**

This program can be used to calculate averages on tests. The program can calculate the average or can calculate the average with the lowest test score dropped.

### **Possible Test Day Questions**

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The purpose of my program is to allow teachers to quickly calculate student averages with and without the lowest grade dropped. By saving time, teachers will have additional class time to cover additional content.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The program functions by asking the user how many test scores to include and then fills up the list at that size by asking the user to input all the student’s grades in a loop. After the list is filled, the program asks the user if she wants to calculate the average with or without the lowest test score dropped. The minimum number in the list is found, and the average is determined with or without the minimum number.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

The user is asked “How many tests.” The user inputs 4.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 90.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 100.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 60.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 80.

The program outputs to the screen “90” and “82.5.”

4.Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data contained in the list testScores are integers that represent a student’s test scores. These test scores are used to calculate the average test score or the average test score with the lowest score dropped, as well as to determine the lowest test score.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently, if you did not use the list.

My list testScores holds four test scores. By using my list, I only had to name the list. If I did not use testScores, I would have to name all four of the tests with their own variable names. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances of error. By reducing the chances of errors, it reduced the level of complexity of my program. By putting my test scores in testScores, I was able to reference and test each score by index using a loop and a single if statement. If I did not use my list, I would have to reference each score by its name and use four different if statements. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances of error. By reducing the chances of errors, it reduced the level of complexity of my program.

Furthermore, by using my list I could store four scores or 100 scores without an additional code. If I did not use my list, I would have to name an additional 96 scores. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances of error. By reducing the chances of errors, it reduced the level of complexity of my program.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

The included procedure uses the user’s input to calculate average. If the user enters “true,” the lowest test grade is dropped from the average. If the user enters “false,” the lowest grade is included in the average. After calculation, the average is returned as output.

7.Describe in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

The procedure first checks the value of a user-entered input (true/false). If the user enters “true,” the procedure iterates through the data structure and accumulates the total value of the test scores. This program starts accumulating from the first to the last indexed test stored in the data structure. The procedure also keeps track of how many tests are in the data structure by adding one to count for every iteration. The procedure then returns the (total score – the lowest test score)/(the number of tests – 1). If the user enters “false,” the procedure iterates through the data structure and accumulates the total value of the test scores. This program starts accumulating from the first to the last indexed test stored in the data structure. The procedure also keeps track of how many tests are in the data structure by adding one to count for every iteration. The procedure then returns the (total score)/(the number of tests).

8.Describe two calls to the procedure identified in written response 3i. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

![Images](OEBPS/images/f109-01.jpg)

The first call to the procedure includes two arguments. The first argument is the list that contains the grades. The second argument is a Boolean value that indicates that the lowest test score should be dropped. The selection statement in the procedure executes as true, and the average is calculated with the lowest score dropped.

Second call:

![Images](OEBPS/images/f109-02.jpg)

The second call to the procedure includes two arguments. The first argument is the list that contains the grades. The second argument is a Boolean value that indicates that the lowest test score should not be dropped.

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

The selection statement in the procedure executes as “true,” and the average is calculated with the lowest score dropped.

The selection statement in the procedure executes as “false,” and the average is calculated with all scores from the data structure.

Identify the result of each call.

First call:

95

Second call:

90

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for the user input times, which in this case was 4 iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

The loop utilized in my program is designed to iterate through the list of tests. The “repeat” loop automatically starts at the first test score and concludes at the last test score, ensuring there is no possibility of an infinite loop. However, if I were to switch to a “while” loop and set it to continue until “x” is less than 4 without ever incrementing “x,” it would result in an infinite loop. This is because “x” will never not become less than 4, leading to an infinite loop.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Attempting to divide by zero would lead to a runtime error. Therefore, if I provided the program with a list that did not contain any test scores, the “count” would be zero. Consequently, when calculating the average and attempting to divide by “count,” which is zero, it would cause a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Modifying the code to drop the lowest score without subtracting “count” by one would lead to an incorrect lower average, resulting in a logical error.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

One of the benefits of a graphical language is it is not possible to have a syntax error. This is due to the pieces of code not fitting together like a puzzle piece. However, trying to place blocks or elements in a way that doesn’t align with the expected structure would attempt to create a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

If the data type of “total” were an 8-bit integer, the maximum value it could hold without triggering an overflow error would be 2^n – 1, which in this specific instance, amounts to 255 (derived from 256 – 1). Consequently, any endeavor to store the number 278 in “total” would unquestionably result in an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction, and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm within a procedure, I have significantly reduced the complexity of my program. This simplification allows me to reuse the algorithm at multiple points in my program without the need for redundant code. For example, when I need to calculate the average score for 15 students, I can simply call the procedure 15 times. Without this procedural approach, I would have to replicate the entire code 15 times instead of just once.

Furthermore, the use of procedures ensures that errors associated with the “scrambleWord” functionality are localized within the procedure itself. Without procedures, I would need to inspect the entire program to pinpoint the source of any errors related to this functionality.

16.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called isEqual(value1, value2). The procedure returns true if the two parameters, value1 and value2, are equal in value and returns false otherwise. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses isEqual to count the number of times a certain value appears in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

**1.Initialization**: Start by initializing the variable “total” with a value of 0.

**2.Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “testScores” list.

**3.Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is compared to a test contained in the list. If a match is found, increase the “total” by one.

**4.Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

![Images](OEBPS/images/f115-01.jpg)

3ii.The second program code segment must show the data in the same list being used, such as creating new data from the existing data or accessing multiple elements in the list, as part of fulfilling the program’s purpose.

![Images](OEBPS/images/f115-02.jpg)

### **Possible Test Day Questions**

1.Describe the overall purpose of the program.

The purpose of my program is to allow teachers to quickly calculate student averages with and without the lowest grade dropped. By saving time, teachers will have additional class time to cover additional content.

2.Describe what functionality of the program is demonstrated in the video.

The program functions by asking the user how many test scores to include. Then, the program fills the list, at that size, by asking the user to input all the student’s grades in a loop. After the list is filled, the program asks the user if he wants to calculate the average with or without the lowest test score dropped. The minimum number in the list is found, and the average is determined with or without the minimum number.

3.Describe the input and output of the program demonstrated in the video.

The user is asked “How many tests?”

The user inputs 4.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 90.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 100.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 60.

The user is asked “Enter test score.”

The user inputs 80.

The program outputs to the screen “90” and “82.5.”

4.Describe what the data contained in the list represent in your program.

The data contained in the testScores list are integers that represent the student’s test scores. These test scores are used to calculate the average test score or the average test score with the lowest score dropped and the lowest test score.

5.Explain how the selected list manages complexity in your program code by explaining why your program code could not be written, or how it would be written differently, if you did not use the list.

My list, testScores, holds four test scores. By using my list, I only had to name the list. If I did not use testScores, I would have to name all four of the tests with their own variable names. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances for error. By reducing the chances for error, it reduced the level of complexity of my program.

By putting my test scores in testScores, I was able to reference and test each score by index using a loop and a single if statement. If I did not use my list, I would have to reference each score by its name and use four different if statements. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances for error. By reducing the chances for error, it reduced the level of complexity of my program. Furthermore, by using my list, I could store four scores or 100 scores without an additional code. If I did not use my list, I would have to name an additional 96 scores. This reduction in required code will reduce the chances for error. By reducing the chances for error, it reduced the level of complexity of my program.

6.Describe, in general, what the identified procedure does and how it contributes to the overall functionality of the program.

The included procedure uses the user’s input to calculate average. If the user enters “true,” the lowest test grade is dropped from the average. If the user enters “false,” the lowest grade is included in the average. After calculation, the average is returned as output.

7.Describes in detailed steps how the algorithm implemented in the identified procedure works. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to recreate it.

The procedure first checks the value of a user-entered input (true/false).

If the user enters “true,” the procedure iterates through the data structure and accumulates the total value of the test scores. This program starts accumulating from the first to the last indexed test stored in the data structure.

The procedure also keeps track of how many tests are in the data structure by adding one to count for every iteration.

The procedure then returns the (total score − the lowest test score)/(the number of tests − 1).

If the user enters “false,” the procedure iterates through the data structure and accumulates the total value of the test scores. This program starts accumulating from the first to the last indexed test stored in the data structure.

The procedure also keeps track of how many tests are in the data structure by adding one to count for every iteration.

The procedure then returns the (total score)/(the number of tests).

8.Describe two calls to the procedure identified in written response. Each call must pass a different argument(s) that causes a different segment of code in the algorithm to execute.

First call:

double average = getAverage(testScores, ‘y’);

The first call to the procedure includes two arguments. The first argument is the list that contains the grades. The second argument is a char value that indicates that the lowest test score should be dropped. The selection statement in the procedure executes as true, and the average is calculated with the lowest score dropped.

Second call:

double averageFull = getAverage(testScores, ‘n’ );

The second call to the procedure includes two arguments. The first argument is the list that contains the grades. The second argument is a char value that indicates that the lowest test score will not be dropped.

Describe what condition(s) is being tested by each call to the procedure.

First call: The selection statement in the procedure executes as “true,” and the average is calculated with the lowest score dropped.

Second call: The selection statement in the procedure executes as “false,” and the average is calculated with all scores from the data structure.

Identify the result of each call.

First call:

95

Second call:

90

9.Identify the number of times the body of your iteration statement will execute.

The loop in my program will run for the user input times, which in this case was four iterations.

10.Describe a condition or error that would cause your iteration statement to not terminate and cause an infinite loop. If no such condition or error exists, explain how the loop could be modified to cause an infinite loop.

The loop in my program is intended to traverse the list of test scores. The “for” loop begins at index 0 and proceeds until it reaches the last index, which is determined by the length of the “testScores” list. If I were to modify the “for” loop and change “x++” to just “x,” it would lead to an infinite loop because “x” would remain at 0 indefinitely.

11.Outline a modification that would result in a runtime error.

Attempting to divide by zero would lead to a runtime error. Therefore, if I provided the program with a list that did not contain any test scores, the “count” would be zero. Consequently, when calculating the average and attempting to divide by “count,” which is zero, it would cause a runtime error.

12.Outline a modification that would result in a logical error.

Modifying the code to drop the lowest score without subtracting “count” by one would lead to an incorrect lower average, resulting in a logical error.

13.Outline a modification that would result in a syntax error.

In Java, it’s relatively common to encounter syntax errors. For instance, forgetting the semicolon in line 30 would lead to a syntax error.

14.Outline a modification that would result in an overflow error.

If the data type of “total” were an 8-bit integer, the maximum value it could hold without triggering an overflow error would be 2^n − 1, which, in this specific instance, amounts to 255 (derived from 256 − 1). Consequently, any endeavor to store the number 278 in “total” would unquestionably result in an overflow error.

15.Elaborate on the role of procedural abstraction within your program. Explain how the program’s structure would differ if it did not use a procedural abstraction, and discuss how employing the procedural abstraction has simplified the program’s complexity.

By encapsulating the algorithm within a procedure, I have significantly reduced the complexity of my program. This simplification allows me to reuse the algorithm at multiple points in my program without the need for redundant code. For example, when I need to calculate the average score for 15 students, I can simply call the procedure 15 times. Without this procedural approach, I would have to replicate the entire code 15 times instead of just once.

Furthermore, the use of procedures ensures that errors associated with the “scrambleWord” functionality are localized within the procedure itself. Without procedures, I would need to inspect the entire program to pinpoint the source of any errors related to this functionality.

16.Suppose you are provided with a procedure called isEqual(value1, value2). The procedure returns true if the two parameters, value1 and value2, are equal in value and returns false otherwise. Using the list you identified in the List section of your Personalized Project Reference, explain in detailed steps an algorithm that uses isEqual to count the number of times a certain value appears in your list. Your explanation must be detailed enough for someone else to write the program code.

**1.Initialization**: Start by initializing the variable “total” with a value of 0.

**2.Iterate Through the List**: Commence the process of iterating through the “testScores” list.

**3.Counting Matches**: For each item in the list, assess if the “user” is compared to a test contained in the list. If a match is found, increase the “total” by one.

**4.Completion**: Once all items in the list have been iterated through, return the accumulated value of “total.”

## [**Computing Innovations**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev14)

Be it the flight of the vulturine guinea fowl, sonar used by whales and dolphins, or the majestic nature of termite architecture, inspiration for computing innovations can be found anywhere. People are the ones who create innovations. Computing innovations are constantly changing and building on themselves. The desire to prevent crime has created countless innovations, such as data mapping, tracking, and biometrics technologies. Not all innovations have been home runs. Many innovations have also been created that have difficulty finding an audience, such as Bluetooth toasters, smart water fountains for cats, and fridges that connect to the internet.

Advances in computing have generated and increased creativity in other fields, such as medicine, engineering, communications, and the arts. Health insurance companies are using data analytics to make decisions about which providers are more appropriate based on quantitative data. Heart monitors can take constant blood pressure and be worn as watches. Artificial legs controlled by microprocessors and automated reading applications for the blind have improved quality of life. Computing innovations can also have an impact on the arts by providing new ways to mix different types of media. Computing simulations can model real-world situations and predict outcomes based on changing variables.

### **Hardware vs. Software**

Hardware is the physical components of a computing device, while software is the instructions in a programming language to the computing device. A computing innovation can have hardware components. However, the computing innovation is about the software, not the hardware.

Computing hardware has gotten smaller and more powerful over the years. Moore’s law predicts that the size of transistors halves every two years while the cost also halves every two years. Computers went from taking up 1,800 square feet and weighing almost 50 tons to being able to fit in your pocket.

Examples:

 
| Software || Hardware |
| --- | --- |
| Operating systems || Motherboard |
| --- | --- |
| Driverless vehicle software to avoid crashes || Self-driving car |
| Dual-monitor programs for Windows || Monitor |
| Compiler || Transistor |
| Graphics card driver || Graphics card |

## [**Collaboration**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev15)

Collaboration helps people learn from each other. Collaboration that includes diverse perspectives helps to avoid bias in the development of computing innovations. For example, if females play video games at the same percentage as males, a game company might not avoid bias if it employed males to write the code for the games. Bringing in female coders could bring additional perspectives that might not have been achieved otherwise. Programming companies often hire people who not only are good programmers but also have interpersonal skills needed to collaborate effectively. Effective collaboration can help one gain insight and knowledge by applying multiple perspectives, experiences, and skill sets.

Collaboration is a learned skill. That skill includes but is not limited to:

■Communication

■Consensus building

■Conflict resolution

■Negotiation

Collaboration with others can make the programmer more self-aware. Group programming can match up your weaknesses with someone else’s strengths, which results in a better product and leads to insight and knowledge not obtainable when working alone.

Collaboration facilitates the solving of computational problems by applying multiple perspectives and skill sets. Collaboration combines different individuals’ resources, talents, and experiences.

![Images](OEBPS/images/fg02_001a.jpg)

**Collaboration**

Collaboration is not limited by location. Current computing tools allow people in different physical locations to share data. Online collaboration tools, such as Google Docs, Zoom, Slack, Yammer, and—by the time you read this—dozens of other tools, allow programmers to collaborate from home or from anywhere that has internet access.

## [**How Programs Function**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev16)

A program is a collection of program statements that performs a specific task when run by a computer. A program is often referred to as software.

![Images](OEBPS/images/fg02_00200.jpg)

**Example of a program**

A code segment refers to a collection of program statements that are part of a program.

\*\*On your AP exam, code will be written in both text-based form and graphical-based form. For the following examples, this book will show both forms.\*\*

A program needs to work for a variety of inputs and situations.

It is difficult to determine if a program works for every condition. Giant software companies with hundreds of top coders can test and release a program with confidence to find out only after the release that the program contains hidden bugs. For example, the following code will work for any list that starts with 0.

If myList ← {0,1,5,6} and the procedure getTotal(myList) is called, the procedure will return 12, which is the correct total.

However, if myList ← {3,1,5,6} and the procedure getTotal(myList) is called, the procedure will return 18, which is not the correct total.

To correct the accumulating problem, the initial value of total must be set to 0, not myList\[1\].

**Text-Based Code:**

![Images](OEBPS/images/f0132-01.jpg)

Same Code Written in Graphical-Based Form:

**Example of a program written in graphical form**

A programmer can never have too many test cases. Logical errors demonstrate why collaboration is so important in programming. Sometimes a programmer has been looking at code for so long that he or she cannot see errors. It is common for a fresh-eyed programmer to spot errors that an entrenched programmer working on code for a long time cannot see.

A complex program can be described broadly by what it does. By keeping the program abstract, the user can focus on just using the program without knowing the details of the code that makes it work. For example, right now I am typing the word “Mississippi” into a program called Microsoft Word. I have no idea how the code works, but I can use the interface. When I spell a word incorrectly like “libry,” I get a red wiggle underneath “libry” with the suggestion to spell the word “library.” I don’t know how the program knows I wanted to spell “library,” but I know how to use the program. This abstraction allows me to concentrate on writing and not worry about how spell-check works.

## [**Program Input**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev17)

Program input is data sent to a computer for processing by a program. Input can come in a variety of forms, such as tactile, audio, visual, or text. For example, a cell phone can convert voice (audio) to text to send a message.

A weather program on your phone could take input in many forms.

![Images](OEBPS/images/f0115-01.jpg)

**Example of input/output**

This weather app was triggered by the user saying (audio) “Hey Phone. . . ,” which would be an example of audio input. This triggering is called an event. The event is the action that supplies input data to a program. Events can be generated when a key is pressed, a mouse is clicked, a program is started, or by any other defined action that affects the flow of execution.

On your Create Performance Task, input can be any form. Mobile CSP has many sensors, such as an accelerometer, GPS, temperature, and many more that can be used as inputs.

## [**Development Process**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev18)

The development process, as shown in the figure below, can be ordered and intentional or can be exploratory in nature.

![Images](OEBPS/images/f0115-02.jpg)

**Development process**

#### Example:

Investigating and reflecting: Due to location restrictions, there is a large need for an effective way of communicating with a team.

Designing: Design the steps needed to develop multiple algorithms that allow for certain functionalities such as video conferencing.

Prototyping: Write the program.

Testing: Execute the program to find errors.

After eliminating errors, adding features may be needed that set the programmers back to the investigating and reflecting step. For example, the video conferencing application may need to reduce background noise by adding a mute feature.

This process will be repeated until an acceptable product is reached or the programmer just runs out of time.

A development process that is incremental is one that breaks the problem into smaller pieces and makes sure each piece works before adding it to the whole. A program rarely works on the first round of development. The larger the program is, the more likely that errors will occur and the more difficult it is to find those errors. To reduce the debugging process, large programs are broken up into small steps.

![Images](OEBPS/images/f0116-01.jpg)

Once they work properly, add the small chunks of code together to form the bigger program.

The design of a program incorporates investigations to determine its requirements. Most programs are designed to be used by people other than the programmers. To meet the needs of the users, the investigation must identify the program constraints as well as the concerns and interests of people who will use the program.

Some ways investigations can be performed are as follows:

■Collecting data through surveys

■User testing

■Interviews

■Direct observations

The design phase of a program may include:

■Brainstorming

■Planning and storyboarding

■Organizing the program into modules and functional components

■Creating diagrams that represent the layouts of the user interface

■Developing a testing strategy for the program

### **Program Documentation**

Program documentation is a written description of the function of a code segment, event, procedure, or program and how it was developed.

Program documentation helps in developing and maintaining correct programs when working individually or in collaborative programming environments. Programmers should document a program throughout its development. Documentation helps the programmer remember what he or she was thinking or the collaborative partners were thinking at the time they were programming.

Comments are a form of program documentation written into the program that do not affect how the program runs. Comments do not affect the run speed of a program.

Program documentation can be used to acknowledge code segments written by someone else. This acknowledgment should include the origin or author’s name. On your Create Performance Task, make sure you document if you use code segments written by someone else.

In this text, I used two backslashes // to indicate everything to the right of the two backslashes // is a comment. The following example shows how comments can appear in both text-based and graphical-based code.

**Text-Based Code:**

![Images](OEBPS/images/p0117-01.jpg)

Sample Code Written in Graphical-Based Form:

![Images](OEBPS/images/f0117-01.jpg)

**Graphical program with documentation**

Documentation can look different depending on what programming language you use. On the Create Performance Task, you can either document in your code or include a separate document with your documentation.

### **Program Errors**

They happen!

Three types of errors can occur:

■Logic error—This is a mistake in the algorithm or program that causes it to behave incorrectly or unexpectedly.

■Syntax error—This is a mistake in the program where the rules of the programming language are not followed.

■Runtime error—This is a mistake in the program that occurs during the execution of a program. Programming languages define their own runtime errors.

## [**Answers Explained**](#04_Contents.xhtml#rlev20)

[1](#11_Chapter02.xhtml#que2-1).**(D)**A computing innovation can have hardware components, but the computing innovation is about the software, not the hardware. Some examples of hardware are transistors, monitors, motherboards, and so on. Some examples of software are TikTok, Snapchat, Facebook, and so on. A computing innovation can be physical (e.g., self-driving car), nonphysical computing software (e.g., picture-editing software), or nonphysical computing (e.g., e-commerce).

[2](#11_Chapter02.xhtml#que2-2).**(A)**Be it the flight of the vulturine guinea fowl, sonar used by whales and dolphins, or the majestic nature of termite architecture, inspiration for computing innovations can be found anywhere. Although many innovations are built on previous technology, some innovations are completely unique. For example, the invention of the internet and resulting e-commerce was unique and disrupted industries.

[3](#11_Chapter02.xhtml#que2-3).**(B)**People are the ones who create innovations. Companies and industry can encourage innovations, but they are made up of people. Previous innovations can inspire new innovations, but people do the creating.

[4](#11_Chapter02.xhtml#que2-4).**(C)**Collaboration does not have to happen in the same space. Collaboration can happen in traditional workspaces as well as on virtual teams. Online collaboration tools, such as Google Docs, allow programmers to collaborate from home or from anywhere, such as one team member working in Orlando, Florida, while the other works in Coldfoot, Alaska. Interpersonal skills, such as communication, consensus building, conflict resolution, and negotiation, are essential skills in most sectors of the professional world.

[5](#11_Chapter02.xhtml#que2-5).**(A)**The definition of a program is a collection of program statements that perform a specific task when run by a computer. Most computer devices require programs to function properly.
